Well, um... that's a good question. I really try to keep up with things, I do. But, um... no, not really. 

I feel terrible about it, honestly. I used to love singing in the choir, and playing pickup basketball with the guys. Well, um... I really hate to admit this. I don't want you to think I'm crazy or, you know, ungrateful for everything I have. But... yes.

It's a little embarrassing to admit, because I don't want to sound lazy, but I'm just so worn out. Just doing simple things, like walking from the bedroom to the kitchen... it feels like I've been out hauling lumber all day. I try to rest, I really do, but sitting down or sleeping just doesn't give me anything back. \n\nI hope it's okay that I came in for that? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by being here.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's been about four months now. I just want to make sure I'm explaining this right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you started feeling tired?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess you could say that. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining, because I know I have a good life and a wonderful wife, but... yeah. Well, um... I guess I have noticed a change, but I try really hard not to let it show too much. I don't want people to think I'm being lazy or ignoring them. 

But, honestly... everything I do just feels so slow lately. It's like my whole body is moving through mud. Even simple things just take so much effort. I really am trying my best to keep up, I promise. I just... I can't seem to make myself move any faster. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right. Is it normal to feel that bogged down?

Well, um... gosh, I really hate to admit this. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, because I know I shouldn't complain. But... yes, I do.

Well, um... restless? No, I don't think so. I mean, I really try to just stay put and not be a bother to anyone.

And I don't even know why, or what started it. I should be stronger than that, shouldn't I? I hope you don't think less of me for saying that. I just... I want to be honest with you so I can get better. Is it normal to feel that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... that's a good question. I really try to keep up with things, I do. But, um... no, not really. \n\nI feel terrible about it, honestly. I used to love singing in the choir, and playing pickup basketball with the guys. And I was always out in the garage working on cars, or, you know, grilling out for the family on the weekends. But lately... I just can't seem to make myself care about any of it. I don't even answer people's texts anymore when they ask me to come out. \n\nI know I should, and I feel so guilty for letting people down. I don't want to seem like a bad friend or a bad husband. It's just... the energy isn't there, and the enjoyment isn't there. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not coming across as selfish or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... weight? I haven't really noticed a big difference, to be honest. I mean, I haven't been stepping on the scale or anything, so I couldn't give you an exact number. \n\nMy wife hasn't said anything about my clothes looking different, and I try to make sure I'm still eating when she cooks, so I don't worry her. I really don't want to add to her plate right now. But as far as I can tell, everything seems pretty much the same in that department. Is that... is it okay that I don't know for sure? Should I be keeping better track of that?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really hate to admit this. Is it... is it common for people to feel that way when they're struggling like this?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Well, um... gosh, I really wish I could say yes. I know how important it is to keep busy and have hobbies, and I really don't want to sound like I'm just giving up or being lazy. 

I used to have lots of things I loved. I always looked forward to choir practice, and playing pickup ball with the guys. I liked working on cars in the garage, and... well, I was always the one grilling for the family on the weekends. I really tried to be involved and do things for everyone. 

But lately... I'm so embarrassed to admit this, because I know it makes me sound like a terrible friend and father... I just can't make myself care enough to do any of it. I don't even show up anymore. The guys will text me about a game or choir, and I... I can't even bring myself to answer their texts. I just leave them hanging. 

It's awful of me, I know. I feel so guilty about it. I really hope they don't think I'm just being rude or ignoring them on purpose. Do you think... I mean, is it common to just lose your spark like that? I'm really hoping to get back to being the guy who grills and sings for them, I promise I am. I hope you don't think too poorly of me for letting all that slide right now.

I try to keep busy, I really do, but... I don't know, maybe I'm just not adjusting well to the quiet? Does that sound like something that could make a person this tired? I just want to make sure I'm explaining this right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you started feeling tired?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess you could say that. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining, because I know I have a good life and a wonderful wife, but... yeah. It's been pretty hard. \n\nI wake up and there's just this... this really heavy, hopeless feeling right here in my chest. I try to push past it, I really do, but... well, some days I end up just going out to the garage and sitting there. And, um, this is really embarrassing to admit, but sometimes I just cry. And I don't even know why, or what started it. I should be stronger than that, shouldn't I? I hope you don't think less of me for saying that. I just... Is it normal to feel that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... that's a good question. I don't even answer people's texts anymore when they ask me to come out. \n\nI know I should, and I feel so guilty for letting people down. I don't want to seem like a bad friend or a bad husband. It's just... the energy isn't there, and the enjoyment isn't there. Does that make sense? I haven't really noticed a big difference, to be honest. I really don't want to add to her plate right now. But as far as I can tell, everything seems pretty much the same in that department. Is that... is it okay that I don't know for sure? Should I be keeping better track of that?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really hate to admit this. I don't want you to think I'm crazy or, you know, ungrateful for everything I have. But... yes. \n\nIt's really hard to say out loud. That things would be easier for everyone if I wasn't here being such a burden. \n\nAnd, um... when I'm alone in the house, it actually really scares me. It's just... the energy isn't there, and the enjoyment isn't there. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not coming across as selfish or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... weight? I haven't really noticed a big difference, to be honest. I mean, I haven't been stepping on the scale or anything, so I couldn't give you an exact number. \n\nMy wife hasn't said anything about my clothes looking different, and I try to make sure I'm still eating when she cooks, so I don't worry her. I really don't want to add to her plate right now. But as far as I can tell, everything seems pretty much the same in that department. Is that... is it okay that I don't know for sure? Should I be keeping better track of that?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really hate to admit this. I don't want you to think I'm crazy or, you know, ungrateful for everything I have. But... yes. \n\nIt's really hard to say out loud. I feel so guilty even thinking it, because I know how much it would hurt my wife. But lately, this... this thought just keeps coming into my head that I should just be dead. That things would be easier for everyone if I wasn't here being such a burden. \n\nAnd, um... when I'm alone in the house, it actually really scares me. Because I start thinking that I might actually do something to make that happen. I try to push it away, I really do, but it just gets so loud sometimes. I hope you don't think I'm a terrible person for having those thoughts. I'm telling you because I want to get better, I promise. Is it... is it common for people to feel that way when they're struggling like this?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I have noticed a change, but I try really hard not to let it show too much. I don't want people to think I'm being lazy or ignoring them. \n\nBut, honestly... everything I do just feels so slow lately. It's like my whole body is moving through mud. Even simple things just take so much effort. And, um... when people talk to me, like my wife, I can hear what she's asking, but it just takes me such a long time to get the words out to answer her. I feel terrible about it, because I know it must be frustrating for her to wait on me. \n\nI really am trying my best to keep up, I promise. I just... I can't seem to make myself move any faster. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right. Is it normal to feel that bogged down?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge most of the time since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... anxious? I wouldn't say I'm feeling on edge all the time, no. I mean, I try really hard to stay calm and not let things get to me. \n\nI know it's not good to be all wound up, and I really don't want to be difficult to be around. So I just try to take deep breaths and keep things peaceful, especially for my wife's sake. I don't think I've been overly nervous or anything like that. \n\nI hope that's a good thing? I mean, I know I'm struggling with the other stuff, but at least I'm not pacing the floors or feeling panicked. That's... that's okay, right? That I don't have that symptom?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or guilty about things that wouldn't normally bother you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... gosh, I really hate to admit this. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, because I know I shouldn't complain. But... yes, I do. \n\nI just... I keep thinking that I've completely failed my wife, and my kids, and my church. It's so embarrassing to say out loud. And the worst part—and I really hope you don't think I'm completely losing my mind when I say this—but it honestly feels like every bad thing happening around us lately is somehow... a punishment. Like I'm being punished for not being the man I was supposed to be for them. \n\nI try really hard to shake it off and be positive so I don't bring everyone else down, I promise I do. It just gets so heavy. Does... does that make sense to you? I hope I'm not sounding too terrible by admitting that. Is it normal to feel that kind of guilt when you're going through a rough patch?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... restless? No, I don't think so. I mean, I really try to just stay put and not be a bother to anyone. \n\nLike I said before, everything just feels so slow and heavy, so I don't really have the energy to be fidgety or pace around. I mostly just sit in my chair. I try to stay out of my wife's way so she doesn't have to worry about me being restless or getting into things. \n\nI hope that's alright? I mean, I know I'm not doing much of anything these days, but at least I'm not being disruptive or hyper, right? I really do try to just keep quiet and still. Is it... is it better that I'm not feeling restless on top of everything else?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating or making decisions since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... concentrating? I mean, maybe a little bit, but I really try my best to stay focused. I don't want to be one of those people who just spaces out when someone is talking to them. I know how rude that can be.\n\nI guess... sometimes when my wife asks me what I want for dinner, or what we should watch on TV, I just tell her whatever she wants is fine. It's not that I can't decide, exactly, it's just... I want her to be happy, you know? I hope I'm not being too much of a burden by being a little forgetful or indecisive. It's not terrible, though, I promise. I can still hold a conversation, like we are now, right? I'm doing okay with your questions?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find yourself looking forward to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... gosh, I really wish I could say yes. I know how important it is to keep busy and have hobbies, and I really don't want to sound like I'm just giving up or being lazy. \n\nI used to have lots of things I loved. I always looked forward to choir practice, and playing pickup ball with the guys. I liked working on cars in the garage, and... well, I was always the one grilling for the family on the weekends. Well, um... no, not really. I mean, I try not to. I know worrying doesn't actually fix anything, and I really don't want to be one of those people who's always fretting and making everyone around them anxious, you know?
